I know it sounds—well, my sister says I’m “carrying too much,” but she didn’t see the way everyone got quiet when I said it. Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. It’s not just people being quiet, though that happens too. It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head… I mean, my thoughts are already such a mess sometimes. But the things around me, yes. At church especially.

When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me. They might act like it’s just part of the service, but I can feel it. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.

And it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. I’d be thinking about nothing more than whether I had cash for the offering plate. But now every word feels pointed at me. Other people don’t seem to notice, but I do.

Yes… I do. It’s mostly at church. Like when the pastor says certain prayers, or the choir sings those lines—about mercy, or judgment, or being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me.

Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. I mean, people say things, and sometimes I wonder, but it’s really at church where it’s clear.

When the pastor prays, or the choir sings those certain lines, I know they’re warning me. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.

I keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.

through Patterson Park, but now I just sit in my chair and none of it feels worth getting dressed for.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Food tastes like cardboard to me, and my skirts have gotten loose because most days I only pick at toast or tea even when my niece puts a plate right in front of what I did wrong, then if I do sleep I’m up before dawn with my heart pounding and can’t settle back down.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest I’d be thinking about nothing more than whether I had cash for the offering plate. But now every word feels pointed at me. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.

It’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.

I still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.

I have also stopped going to the church groups and visits that used to keep me connected.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, ever since my close friend died.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / cashier / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S030\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like events or actions around you, like what people say or do, are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s mostly at church. Like when the pastor says certain prayers, or the choir sings those lines—about mercy, or judgment, or being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me.\n\nI used to just listen to the hymns, you know, and think about whether I had my purse zipped or if I’d turned the stove off before leaving… little things like that. But now it feels different. Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. I mean, people say things, and sometimes I wonder, but it’s really at church where it’s clear.\n\nWhen the pastor prays, or the choir sings those certain lines, I know they’re warning me. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.\n\nIt’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written somewhere—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… not really from the TV or newspapers. I mean, sometimes the television is on while I’m making tea or folding things, but it’s just noise.

It’s at church where I know. When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… about mercy, judgment, being washed clean… that’s when it feels clear. They’re warning me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.

I know other people probably just hear a hymn, but I hear what it means.

I'm Dr. [Your Name]. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I... I came because my family keeps saying I need to talk to somebody, but they don’t understand. I keep telling them it’s my fault my friend died. I didn’t call her back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and that matters, you know? People act like thoughts don’t count, but the Lord knows.\n\nAnd now I know He’s punishing everybody at church because of me. I see it. I told them, but they just get quiet or try to change the subject. My friend used to sit near the back pew, by the flowers—well, they moved those flowers last Easter, I think, because Mrs. Harlan said they were blocking the aisle—but that’s not the point. The point is I did this. I brought this on everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or sounds that others don’t seem to hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t... I don’t know. Sometimes houses make noises, you know, old pipes and the refrigerator humming, and at church there’s always someone coughing or whispering with the bulletin paper crinkling. I’m not sure what you mean by voices. I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that, not exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions have caused events to happen in a way that others don’t understand, like a punishment from God?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes, that’s what I’ve been trying to tell them. I know people say it doesn’t work that way, but they don’t know what was in my heart. I didn’t call my friend back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes—mean, selfish thoughts—and then she died.\n\nAnd now at church… things feel wrong, like the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I know it sounds—well, my sister says I’m “carrying too much,” but she didn’t see the way everyone got quiet when I said it. They just looked down at the carpet, that old green carpet they keep saying they’ll replace. But I know. I’m the reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain things happening around you, like people being quiet or moving flowers, are directly connected to your thoughts or actions in a way that others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. It’s not just people being quiet, though that happens too. It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.\n\nI used to like the choir, especially when Mrs. Dobbins sang alto—she has that shaky voice but it’s sweet—and I’d stand there with the hymnal, but now I can’t hardly look up. Because I know God has marked me. And the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people don’t want to say it out loud, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like prayers or hymns, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s it. When the pastor prays, sometimes he’ll say something about mercy or being forgiven, and I know he means me, even if he doesn’t look right at me. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. Others don’t understand, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head… I mean, my thoughts are already such a mess sometimes. At church especially.\n\nWhen the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me. They might act like it’s just part of the service, but I can feel it. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nAnd it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. No… not in the news or a book, I don’t think. I don’t read much besides the sale papers sometimes, and even those I just look at the prices, you know, because everything’s gotten so high.

It’s not like a television program is about me. It’s… it’s at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings those lines, I know they’re warning me. About being marked, and judgment, and how the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.

I'm Dr. [Your Name]. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I... I came because my family keeps saying I need to talk to somebody, but they don’t understand. I keep telling them it’s my fault my friend died. I didn’t call her back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and that matters, you know? I told them, but they just get quiet or try to change the subject. My friend used to sit near the back pew, by the flowers—well, they moved those flowers last Easter, I think, because Mrs. Harlan said they were blocking the aisle—but that’s not the point. The point is I did this. I brought this on everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or sounds that others don’t seem to hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t... I don’t know. Sometimes houses make noises, you know, old pipes and the refrigerator humming, and at church there’s always someone coughing or whispering with the bulletin paper crinkling. I’m not sure what you mean by voices. I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that, not exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions have caused events to happen in a way that others don’t understand, like a punishment from God?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes, that’s what I’ve been trying to tell them. I know people say it doesn’t work that way, but they don’t know what was in my heart. I didn’t call my friend back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes—mean, selfish thoughts—and then she died.\n\nAnd now at church… things feel wrong, like the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I know it sounds—well, my sister says I’m “carrying too much,” but she didn’t see the way everyone got quiet when I said it. They just looked down at the carpet, that old green carpet they keep saying they’ll replace. But I know. I’m the reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain things happening around you, like people being quiet or moving flowers, are directly connected to your thoughts or actions in a way that others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. It’s not just people being quiet, though that happens too. It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.\n\nI used to like the choir, especially when Mrs. Dobbins sang alto—she has that shaky voice but it’s sweet—and I’d stand there with the hymnal, but now I can’t hardly look up. Because I know God has marked me. And the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people don’t want to say it out loud, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like prayers or hymns, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s it. When the pastor prays, sometimes he’ll say something about mercy or being forgiven, and I know he means me, even if he doesn’t look right at me. And the hymns too, certain lines about judgment or being washed clean… they’re not just words on a page.\n\nI used to know all those hymns by heart, standing there with the bulletin folded in my purse because I never liked holding too many papers—my hands get dry, you know—but now I hear those lines and I feel cold. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. Others don’t understand, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head… I mean, my thoughts are already such a mess sometimes. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nAnd it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of church… I don’t know if I’d say that. I mean, people say things all the time, and most of it is just people talking, like at the store when someone complains about coupons or the price of eggs—I used to hear that all day, you know.

But at church, that’s different. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… I know that’s not just ordinary. It’s a warning to me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.

I know it sounds… I don’t know. But that’s where I understand it. In the prayers and the singing.

I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that, not exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions have caused events to happen in a way that others don’t understand, like a punishment from God?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes, that’s what I’ve been trying to tell them. I know people say it doesn’t work that way, but they don’t know what was in my heart. I didn’t call my friend back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes—mean, selfish thoughts—and then she died.\n\nAnd now at church… things feel wrong, like the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I know it sounds—well, my sister says I’m “carrying too much,” but she didn’t see the way everyone got quiet when I said it. They just looked down at the carpet, that old green carpet they keep saying they’ll replace. But I know. I’m the reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain things happening around you, like people being quiet or moving flowers, are directly connected to your thoughts or actions in a way that others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. It’s not just people being quiet, though that happens too. It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.\n\nI used to like the choir, especially when Mrs. Dobbins sang alto—she has that shaky voice but it’s sweet—and I’d stand there with the hymnal, but now I can’t hardly look up. Because I know God has marked me. And the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people don’t want to say it out loud, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like prayers or hymns, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s it. When the pastor prays, sometimes he’ll say something about mercy or being forgiven, and I know he means me, even if he doesn’t look right at me. And the hymns too, certain lines about judgment or being washed clean… they’re not just words on a page.\n\nI used to know all those hymns by heart, standing there with the bulletin folded in my purse because I never liked holding too many papers—my hands get dry, you know—but now I hear those lines and I feel cold. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. Others don’t understand, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head… I mean, my thoughts are already such a mess sometimes. But the things around me, yes. At church especially.\n\nWhen the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me. They might act like it’s just part of the service, but I can feel it. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nAnd it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. I’d be thinking about nothing more than whether I had cash for the offering plate. But now every word feels pointed at me. Other people don’t seem to notice, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like events or actions around you, like what people say or do, are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s mostly at church. Like when the pastor says certain prayers, or the choir sings those lines—about mercy, or judgment, or being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me.\n\nI used to just listen to the hymns, you know, and think about whether I had my purse zipped or if I’d turned the stove off before leaving… little things like that. But now it feels different. Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. I mean, people say things, and sometimes I wonder, but it’s really at church where it’s clear.\n\nWhen the pastor prays, or the choir sings those certain lines, I know they’re warning me. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.\n\nIt’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written somewhere—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… not really from the TV or newspapers. I mean, sometimes the television is on while I’m making tea or folding things, but it’s just noise.\n\nIt’s at church where I know. When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… about mercy, judgment, being washed clean… that’s when it feels clear. They’re warning me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know other people probably just hear a hymn, but I hear what it means.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something in the news, on TV, or in a book was specifically about you or meant just for you, even if it wasn’t connected to church?\n\nPatient: No… not in the news or a book, I don’t think. I don’t read much besides the sale papers sometimes, and even those I just look at the prices, you know, because everything’s gotten so high.\n\nIt’s not like a television program is about me. It’s… it’s at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings those lines, I know they’re warning me. About being marked, and judgment, and how the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nThat’s where I hear it. Not the news. The hymns and the prayers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening to you, like what people say or do, are part of a larger plan or have a hidden meaning that only you can understand, even outside of church?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know if I’d say that. I mean, people say things all the time, and most of it is just people talking, like at the store when someone complains about coupons or the price of eggs—I used to hear that all day, you know.\n\nBut at church, that’s different. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… I know that’s not just ordinary. It’s a warning to me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know it sounds… I don’t know. But that’s where I understand it. In the prayers and the singing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know it sounds—well, my sister says I’m “carrying too much,” but she didn’t see the way everyone got quiet when I said it. They just looked down at the carpet, that old green carpet they keep saying they’ll replace. But I know. I’m the reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain things happening around you, like people being quiet or moving flowers, are directly connected to your thoughts or actions in a way that others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. It’s not just people being quiet, though that happens too. It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.\n\nI used to like the choir, especially when Mrs. Dobbins sang alto—she has that shaky voice but it’s sweet—and I’d stand there with the hymnal, but now I can’t hardly look up. Because I know God has marked me. And the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people don’t want to say it out loud, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like prayers or hymns, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s it. When the pastor prays, sometimes he’ll say something about mercy or being forgiven, and I know he means me, even if he doesn’t look right at me. And the hymns too, certain lines about judgment or being washed clean… they’re not just words on a page.\n\nI used to know all those hymns by heart, standing there with the bulletin folded in my purse because I never liked holding too many papers—my hands get dry, you know—but now I hear those lines and I feel cold. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. Others don’t understand, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head… I mean, my thoughts are already such a mess sometimes. But the things around me, yes. At church especially.\n\nWhen the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me. They might act like it’s just part of the service, but I can feel it. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nAnd it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. I’d be thinking about nothing more than whether I had cash for the offering plate. But now every word feels pointed at me. Other people don’t seem to notice, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like events or actions around you, like what people say or do, are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s mostly at church. Like when the pastor says certain prayers, or the choir sings those lines—about mercy, or judgment, or being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me.\n\nI used to just listen to the hymns, you know, and think about whether I had my purse zipped or if I’d turned the stove off before leaving… little things like that. But now it feels different. Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. I mean, people say things, and sometimes I wonder, but it’s really at church where it’s clear.\n\nWhen the pastor prays, or the choir sings those certain lines, I know they’re warning me. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.\n\nIt’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written somewhere—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… not really from the TV or newspapers. I mean, sometimes the television is on while I’m making tea or folding things, but it’s just noise.\n\nIt’s at church where I know. When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… about mercy, judgment, being washed clean… that’s when it feels clear. They’re warning me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know other people probably just hear a hymn, but I hear what it means.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something in the news, on TV, or in a book was specifically about you or meant just for you, even if it wasn’t connected to church?\n\nPatient: No… not in the news or a book, I don’t think. I don’t read much besides the sale papers sometimes, and even those I just look at the prices, you know, because everything’s gotten so high.\n\nIt’s not like a television program is about me. It’s… it’s at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings those lines, I know they’re warning me. About being marked, and judgment, and how the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nThat’s where I hear it. Not the news. The hymns and the prayers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening to you, like what people say or do, are part of a larger plan or have a hidden meaning that only you can understand, even outside of church?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know if I’d say that. I mean, people say things all the time, and most of it is just people talking, like at the store when someone complains about coupons or the price of eggs—I used to hear that all day, you know.\n\nBut at church, that’s different. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… I know that’s not just ordinary. It’s a warning to me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know it sounds… I don’t know. But that’s where I understand it. In the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do in places other than church—like at the store, at home, or while watching TV—are part of a message meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Not really at the store or at home, no… I mean, at home the TV is just on sometimes, and half the time I’m not even listening. And the store, well, people just talk, complain about prices, the lines, all that.\n\nIt’s church where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain parts… I know they’re warning me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us. It’s not just words to me there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening to you outside of church, like something at home or in public, are part of a message or have a meaning that only you understand?\n\nPatient: Not outside church so much… I mean, at home there’s always something going on, dishes in the sink, mail piling up, and I used to be good about keeping up with all that before things got so heavy.\n\nBut no, not like a special message at the store or anything. It’s when I’m at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings certain lines, that’s when I know it’s meant for me. They’re warning me… that God has marked me, and that the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… I know that’s not just ordinary. It’s a warning to me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know it sounds… I don’t know. But that’s where I understand it. In the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do in places other than church—like at the store, at home, or while watching TV—are part of a message meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Not really at the store or at home, no… I mean, at home the TV is just on sometimes, and half the time I’m not even listening. And the store, well, people just talk, complain about prices, the lines, all that.\n\nIt’s church where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain parts… I know they’re warning me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us. It’s not just words to me there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening to you outside of church, like something at home or in public, are part of a message or have a meaning that only you understand?\n\nPatient: Not outside church so much… I mean, at home there’s always something going on, dishes in the sink, mail piling up, and I used to be good about keeping up with all that before things got so heavy.\n\nBut no, not like a special message at the store or anything. It’s when I’m at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings certain lines, that’s when I know it’s meant for me. They’re warning me… that God has marked me, and that the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel that events or actions happening around you, like what people say or do, are directed at you or have a special meaning for you in places other than church, like at home, in the store, or while watching TV?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / cashier / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"When the pastor says the prayers or the choir sings certain lines, I know they are really warning me that God has marked me and that the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really in those places… I mean, the TV says all kinds of foolishness, and at the store people just grumble, same as they always did when I was working the register. Folks would get so upset over a coupon not scanning, and I’d have to stand there smiling like my feet weren’t killing me.

But… church is different. That’s where I feel it. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines, I know it’s not just regular words. It’s a warning to me. Like God has marked me somehow, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.

I'm Dr. [Your Name]. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I... I came because my family keeps saying I need to talk to somebody, but they don’t understand. I keep telling them it’s my fault my friend died. I didn’t call her back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and that matters, you know? People act like thoughts don’t count, but the Lord knows.\n\nAnd now I know He’s punishing everybody at church because of me. I see it. I told them, but they just get quiet or try to change the subject. My friend used to sit near the back pew, by the flowers—well, they moved those flowers last Easter, I think, because Mrs. Harlan said they were blocking the aisle—but that’s not the point. The point is I did this. I brought this on everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or sounds that others don’t seem to hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t... I don’t know. Sometimes houses make noises, you know, old pipes and the refrigerator humming, and at church there’s always someone coughing or whispering with the bulletin paper crinkling. I’m not sure what you mean by voices. I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that, not exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions have caused events to happen in a way that others don’t understand, like a punishment from God?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes, that’s what I’ve been trying to tell them. I know people say it doesn’t work that way, but they don’t know what was in my heart. I didn’t call my friend back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes—mean, selfish thoughts—and then she died.\n\nAnd now at church… things feel wrong, like the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I know it sounds—well, my sister says I’m “carrying too much,” but she didn’t see the way everyone got quiet when I said it. They just looked down at the carpet, that old green carpet they keep saying they’ll replace. But I know. I’m the reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain things happening around you, like people being quiet or moving flowers, are directly connected to your thoughts or actions in a way that others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. It’s not just people being quiet, though that happens too. It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.\n\nI used to like the choir, especially when Mrs. Dobbins sang alto—she has that shaky voice but it’s sweet—and I’d stand there with the hymnal, but now I can’t hardly look up. Because I know God has marked me. And the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people don’t want to say it out loud, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like prayers or hymns, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s it. When the pastor prays, sometimes he’ll say something about mercy or being forgiven, and I know he means me, even if he doesn’t look right at me. And the hymns too, certain lines about judgment or being washed clean… they’re not just words on a page.\n\nI used to know all those hymns by heart, standing there with the bulletin folded in my purse because I never liked holding too many papers—my hands get dry, you know—but now I hear those lines and I feel cold. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. Others don’t understand, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head… I mean, my thoughts are already such a mess sometimes. But the things around me, yes. At church especially.\n\nWhen the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me. They might act like it’s just part of the service, but I can feel it. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nAnd it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. I’d be thinking about nothing more than whether I had cash for the offering plate. But now every word feels pointed at me. Other people don’t seem to notice, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like events or actions around you, like what people say or do, are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s mostly at church. Like when the pastor says certain prayers, or the choir sings those lines—about mercy, or judgment, or being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me.\n\nI used to just listen to the hymns, you know, and think about whether I had my purse zipped or if I’d turned the stove off before leaving… little things like that. But now it feels different. Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. I mean, people say things, and sometimes I wonder, but it’s really at church where it’s clear.\n\nWhen the pastor prays, or the choir sings those certain lines, I know they’re warning me. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.\n\nIt’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written somewhere—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… not really from the TV or newspapers. I mean, sometimes the television is on while I’m making tea or folding things, but it’s just noise.\n\nIt’s at church where I know. When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… about mercy, judgment, being washed clean… that’s when it feels clear. They’re warning me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know other people probably just hear a hymn, but I hear what it means.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something in the news, on TV, or in a book was specifically about you or meant just for you, even if it wasn’t connected to church?\n\nPatient: No… not in the news or a book, I don’t think. It’s… it’s at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings those lines, I know they’re warning me. About being marked, and judgment, and how the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nThat’s where I hear it. Not the news. The hymns and the prayers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening to you, like what people say or do, are part of a larger plan or have a hidden meaning that only you can understand, even outside of church?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know if I’d say that. Well… outside church, I don’t really notice it the same way. At home it’s mostly just the television mumbling on, and I’ll be thinking about laundry or what I forgot at the market, and then I lose track.

It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.\n\nI used to like the choir, especially when Mrs. Dobbins sang alto—she has that shaky voice but it’s sweet—and I’d stand there with the hymnal, but now I can’t hardly look up. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nAnd it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. But now it feels different. Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.\n\nIt’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.\n\nI used to like the choir, especially when Mrs. Dobbins sang alto—she has that shaky voice but it’s sweet—and I’d stand there with the hymnal, but now I can’t hardly look up. Because I know God has marked me. And the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people don’t want to say it out loud, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like prayers or hymns, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s it. When the pastor prays, sometimes he’ll say something about mercy or being forgiven, and I know he means me, even if he doesn’t look right at me. And the hymns too, certain lines about judgment or being washed clean… they’re not just words on a page.\n\nI used to know all those hymns by heart, standing there with the bulletin folded in my purse because I never liked holding too many papers—my hands get dry, you know—but now I hear those lines and I feel cold. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. Others don’t understand, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head… I mean, my thoughts are already such a mess sometimes. But the things around me, yes. At church especially.\n\nWhen the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me. They might act like it’s just part of the service, but I can feel it. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nAnd it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. I’d be thinking about nothing more than whether I had cash for the offering plate. But now every word feels pointed at me. Other people don’t seem to notice, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like events or actions around you, like what people say or do, are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s mostly at church. Like when the pastor says certain prayers, or the choir sings those lines—about mercy, or judgment, or being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me.\n\nI used to just listen to the hymns, you know, and think about whether I had my purse zipped or if I’d turned the stove off before leaving… little things like that. But now it feels different. Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. I mean, people say things, and sometimes I wonder, but it’s really at church where it’s clear.\n\nWhen the pastor prays, or the choir sings those certain lines, I know they’re warning me. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.\n\nIt’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written somewhere—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… not really from the TV or newspapers. I mean, sometimes the television is on while I’m making tea or folding things, but it’s just noise.\n\nIt’s at church where I know. When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… about mercy, judgment, being washed clean… that’s when it feels clear. They’re warning me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know other people probably just hear a hymn, but I hear what it means.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something in the news, on TV, or in a book was specifically about you or meant just for you, even if it wasn’t connected to church?\n\nPatient: No… not in the news or a book, I don’t think. I don’t read much besides the sale papers sometimes, and even those I just look at the prices, you know, because everything’s gotten so high.\n\nIt’s not like a television program is about me. It’s… it’s at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings those lines, I know they’re warning me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know it sounds… I don’t know. But that’s where I understand it. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written somewhere—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… not really from the TV or newspapers. I mean, sometimes the television is on while I’m making tea or folding things, but it’s just noise.\n\nIt’s at church where I know. When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… about mercy, judgment, being washed clean… that’s when it feels clear. They’re warning me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know other people probably just hear a hymn, but I hear what it means.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something in the news, on TV, or in a book was specifically about you or meant just for you, even if it wasn’t connected to church?\n\nPatient: No… not in the news or a book, I don’t think. Like God has marked me somehow, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening around you, like what people say or do, are directed at you or have a special meaning for you outside of church, such as at home, in the store, or while watching TV?\n\nPatient: Well… outside church, I don’t really notice it the same way. At home it’s mostly just the television mumbling on, and I’ll be thinking about laundry or what I forgot at the market, and then I lose track.\n\nBut at church… that’s different. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings certain lines, I know it’s meant as a warning. Not just regular words. It’s like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening around you, like what people say or do, are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you, outside of the religious setting?\n\nPatient: Outside of church? No… I don’t think so. At home it’s just the usual things, you know, the sink, the bills, little noises in the house. I get distracted by all that.\n\nBut at church, that’s where it happens. When the pastor prays, or the choir sings certain lines… I know those words are for me. They’re warning me. Like God has marked me, and somehow the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that events or actions happening around you, like what people say or do, have a special meaning for you outside of church, such as at home, in the store, or while watching TV?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / cashier / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"When the pastor says the prayers or the choir sings certain lines, I know they are really warning me that God has marked me and that the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of church… no, not like that. At home I might have the TV on, but half the time I’m not even listening, just thinking about whether I paid the light bill or if I left something in the washer.

But in church, that’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… I know it’s really a warning for me. Like God has marked me somehow, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F32.3, F33.3, F31.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Your Name]. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I... I came because my family keeps saying I need to talk to somebody, but they don’t understand. I keep telling them it’s my fault my friend died. I didn’t call her back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and that matters, you know? People act like thoughts don’t count, but the Lord knows.\n\nAnd now I know He’s punishing everybody at church because of me. I see it. I told them, but they just get quiet or try to change the subject. My friend used to sit near the back pew, by the flowers—well, they moved those flowers last Easter, I think, because Mrs. Harlan said they were blocking the aisle—but that’s not the point. The point is I did this. I brought this on everyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or sounds that others don’t seem to hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t... I don’t know. Sometimes houses make noises, you know, old pipes and the refrigerator humming, and at church there’s always someone coughing or whispering with the bulletin paper crinkling. I’m not sure what you mean by voices. I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that, not exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions have caused events to happen in a way that others don’t understand, like a punishment from God?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes, that’s what I’ve been trying to tell them. I know people say it doesn’t work that way, but they don’t know what was in my heart. I didn’t call my friend back, and I had ugly thoughts sometimes—mean, selfish thoughts—and then she died.\n\nAnd now at church… things feel wrong, like the Lord is punishing everybody there because of me. I know it sounds—well, my sister says I’m “carrying too much,” but she didn’t see the way everyone got quiet when I said it. They just looked down at the carpet, that old green carpet they keep saying they’ll replace. But I know. I’m the reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain things happening around you, like people being quiet or moving flowers, are directly connected to your thoughts or actions in a way that others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s what I mean. It’s not just people being quiet, though that happens too. It’s when the pastor says certain prayers, or when the choir sings those lines about being washed clean or judgment or mercy… I know they’re not just singing regular hymns. They’re warning me.\n\nI used to like the choir, especially when Mrs. Dobbins sang alto—she has that shaky voice but it’s sweet—and I’d stand there with the hymnal, but now I can’t hardly look up. Because I know God has marked me. And the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people don’t want to say it out loud, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like prayers or hymns, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s it. When the pastor prays, sometimes he’ll say something about mercy or being forgiven, and I know he means me, even if he doesn’t look right at me. And the hymns too, certain lines about judgment or being washed clean… they’re not just words on a page.\n\nI used to know all those hymns by heart, standing there with the bulletin folded in my purse because I never liked holding too many papers—my hands get dry, you know—but now I hear those lines and I feel cold. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. Others don’t understand, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being placed in your mind that aren’t yours, or that events or actions around you are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in my head… I mean, my thoughts are already such a mess sometimes. But the things around me, yes. At church especially.\n\nWhen the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me. They might act like it’s just part of the service, but I can feel it. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nAnd it’s funny, I used to sit in the same pew most Sundays, the one with that little crack in the wood where my sleeve would catch sometimes. I’d be thinking about nothing more than whether I had cash for the offering plate. But now every word feels pointed at me. Other people don’t seem to notice, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like events or actions around you, like what people say or do, are specifically directed at you in a way that others don’t seem to notice or understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s mostly at church. Like when the pastor says certain prayers, or the choir sings those lines—about mercy, or judgment, or being washed clean—I know they’re really warning me.\n\nI used to just listen to the hymns, you know, and think about whether I had my purse zipped or if I’d turned the stove off before leaving… little things like that. But now it feels different. Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. I mean, people say things, and sometimes I wonder, but it’s really at church where it’s clear.\n\nWhen the pastor prays, or the choir sings those certain lines, I know they’re warning me. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.\n\nIt’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written somewhere—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… not really from the TV or newspapers. I mean, sometimes the television is on while I’m making tea or folding things, but it’s just noise.\n\nIt’s at church where I know. When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… about mercy, judgment, being washed clean… that’s when it feels clear. They’re warning me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know other people probably just hear a hymn, but I hear what it means.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something in the news, on TV, or in a book was specifically about you or meant just for you, even if it wasn’t connected to church?\n\nPatient: No… not in the news or a book, I don’t think. I don’t read much besides the sale papers sometimes, and even those I just look at the prices, you know, because everything’s gotten so high.\n\nIt’s not like a television program is about me. It’s… it’s at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings those lines, I know they’re warning me. About being marked, and judgment, and how the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nThat’s where I hear it. Not the news. The hymns and the prayers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening to you, like what people say or do, are part of a larger plan or have a hidden meaning that only you can understand, even outside of church?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know if I’d say that. I mean, people say things all the time, and most of it is just people talking, like at the store when someone complains about coupons or the price of eggs—I used to hear that all day, you know.\n\nBut at church, that’s different. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… I know that’s not just ordinary. It’s a warning to me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know it sounds… I don’t know. But that’s where I understand it. In the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do in places other than church—like at the store, at home, or while watching TV—are part of a message meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Not really at the store or at home, no… I mean, at home the TV is just on sometimes, and half the time I’m not even listening. And the store, well, people just talk, complain about prices, the lines, all that.\n\nIt’s church where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain parts… I know they’re warning me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us. It’s not just words to me there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening to you outside of church, like something at home or in public, are part of a message or have a meaning that only you understand?\n\nPatient: Not outside church so much… I mean, at home there’s always something going on, dishes in the sink, mail piling up, and I used to be good about keeping up with all that before things got so heavy.\n\nBut no, not like a special message at the store or anything. It’s when I’m at church. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings certain lines, that’s when I know it’s meant for me. They’re warning me… that God has marked me, and that the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening around you, like what people say or do, are directed at you or have a special meaning for you in places other than church, like at home, in the store, or while watching TV?\n\nPatient: No, not really in those places… I mean, the TV says all kinds of foolishness, and at the store people just grumble, same as they always did when I was working the register. Folks would get so upset over a coupon not scanning, and I’d have to stand there smiling like my feet weren’t killing me.\n\nBut… church is different. That’s where I feel it. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines, I know it’s not just regular words. It’s a warning to me. Like God has marked me somehow, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening around you, like what people say or do, are directed at you or have a special meaning for you outside of church, such as at home, in the store, or while watching TV?\n\nPatient: Well… outside church, I don’t really notice it the same way. At home it’s mostly just the television mumbling on, and I’ll be thinking about laundry or what I forgot at the market, and then I lose track.\n\nBut at church… that’s different. When the pastor is praying, or the choir sings certain lines, I know it’s meant as a warning. Not just regular words. It’s like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening around you, like what people say or do, are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you, outside of the religious setting?\n\nPatient: Outside of church? When the pastor prays, or the choir sings certain lines… I know those words are for me. They’re warning me. Like God has marked me, and somehow the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events or actions happening around you, like what people say or do, have a special meaning for you outside of church, such as at home, in the store, or while watching TV?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… no, not like that. I told them, but they just get quiet or try to change the subject. I’m not sure what you mean by voices. Because I know God has marked me. And the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people don’t want to say it out loud, but I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like prayers or hymns, are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s it. Like God has marked me. Like they’re warning me that the church is suffering because of what I brought on them. But now it feels different. Like those words are meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. Other people may not hear it that way, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church are directed at you or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know. I don’t notice it the same way at the grocery store or when people are just talking. I mean, people say things, and sometimes I wonder, but it’s really at church where it’s clear.\n\nWhen the pastor prays, or the choir sings those certain lines, I know they’re warning me. About being forgiven, or judged, or washed clean… those words feel meant for me. Like God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on them.\n\nI keep thinking about it even when I’m back home, when I’m folding towels or rinsing a coffee cup, but the message comes from there, from the prayers and the hymns.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random events or everyday occurrences outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written in a newspaper—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: TV and newspapers… no, not really like that. I don’t sit there thinking the weatherman is talking to me or anything.\n\nIt’s more at church. That’s where it happens. When the pastor says the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines—about mercy, judgment, being washed clean—it’s like… I know that’s the warning. I know it’s meant for me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI still think about it later, like if I’m at home doing dishes or sorting laundry, and sometimes the TV is on in the background, but the message isn’t from the TV. It comes from the prayers and the singing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people say or do outside of church—like something on TV, a conversation you overhear, or something written somewhere—are sending you messages or have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… not really from the TV or newspapers. I mean, sometimes the television is on while I’m making tea or folding things, but it’s just noise.\n\nIt’s at church where I know. When the pastor says those prayers, or the choir sings certain lines… about mercy, judgment, being washed clean… that’s when it feels clear. They’re warning me. God has marked me, and the church is suffering because of what I brought on us.\n\nI know other people probably just hear a hymn, but I hear what it means.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something in the news, on TV, or in a book was specifically about you or meant just for you, even if it wasn’t connected to church?\n\nPatient: No… not in the news or a book, I don’t think. The hymns and the prayers.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events or actions happening to you, like what people say or do, are part of a larger plan or have a hidden meaning that only you can understand, even outside of church?\n\nPatient: Outside of church… I don’t know if I’d say that.
